## Kaortic hulk

Kaortic hulks were a mutated species of warped beasts from the Far Realm that served the kaorti, albeit reluctantly.

Description
Kaortic hulks barely resembled the terrestrial beasts they were assumed to have mutated from, possessing bronze resinous armor, sharp claws, and no eyes or ears. Nonetheless, they had a keen sense for their prey, and their sightless nature did not deter them from hunting ruthlessly.

Personality
Kaortic hulks were little more than cunning hunters, but their hunger was truly insatiable. A single kaortic hulk was said to be able to decimate entire populations by itself.

Abilities
Kaortic hulks innately possessed a few magical abilities: twice each day, they could replicate the spells silence, invisibility, gaseous form, and spider climb. However, their innate spellcasting was instinctual rather than based on their intellect.

Combat
The kaortic hulk was a bruiser first and foremost, but their large size did not preclude them from being stealthy. Kaortic hulks were known to stalk their prey by utilizing the silence and invisibility spells to deadly effect, then lash out with their claws. Additionally, they could cast gaseous form to enter spaces that their bodies normally could not fit through and spider climb to crawl above their victims and ambush them.

Relationships
Kaortic hulks were conscripted, alongside rukanyrs and skybleeders, to be the ground troops of the kaorti's armies, but they had a temperamental relationship to their so-called masters. If summoned or enthralled by a spell or effect, they had a small, but ever-present, chance to turn on their summoner or master.

History
Occasionally, beasts from the Material Plane would traverse into the Far Realm, but they would not remain in their current form for long. Instead, they were swallowed by the Amoebic Sea, stretched by some eldritch entity, and twisted into a horrifying mockery of their former shape. Some legends stated that the first kaortic hulk was the cat familiar of a mage that was lost to the Far Realm during an attempt to study its properties.With the kaorti established to be transformed Imaskari wizards who explored the Far Realm, and kaortic hulks serving them, it is possible this cat familiar belonged to one of these wizards. However, it is equally possible this cat and mage came from somewhere else, even another world, with the kaortic hulks later being adopted by the kaorti.

## Karmandon

Karmandon was a Netherese rogue and fashionista who owned Karmandon's Finery in Karsus Enclave until its destruction in .

Personality
Karmandon was a skilled and relentless pathological liar. He was particularly adept at verbally backstabbing his own associates, then denying all wrongdoing through a combination of logical reasoning and rhetorical agility. Despite these efforts, he was still well-known as an prolific liar.

Activities
Karmandon sold fashionable clothing, sets of jewelry, and other accoutrements, but his shop started to shrink with the rise of Chanragoupta's Exotic. Unable to compete in the field of business, he turned to vicious rumor-mongering in the hopes of destroying his rival's reputation.

## Kaet Ling

Kaet Ling was a ruthless businesswoman from the Lower City of Baldur's Gate in late 15 century DR.

Description
Kaet Ling was a human woman with hazel eyes, reddish-brown hair gathered in a bun, and sun-kissed golden skin.

History
In the, as the city of Baldur's Gate shuddered under the Absolute crisis, certain goods started to become rare and expensive. One such material that skyrocketed in price was saltpeter. Kaet sought to purchase a shipment of barreled saltpeter from the dwarvish trader Djingo and participated in a bidding war against Smiley Gheef and Pheebs Splayfoot. Djingo charged at least 500 gold pieces per barrel, a high price as he knew that once Baldur's Gate was besieged, he'd easily sell it for double that amount to the foundry. Smiley Gheef offered 700 gp per barrel, but the bidding ward ended abruptly when Kaet Ling offered to pay 1,000 gp per barrel, in cash, no notes.

## Kapenta

Kapenta was a species of edible sprat fish that inhabited most of the inland waterways of Chult in southwest Faerûn.

These fishes had a light body decorated with dark vertical stripes.

Ecology
Kapenta were found in abundance in the waters of the Tiryki and Soshenstar rivers and as such, they were one of the most common freshwater fish consumed by the inhabitants of Port Nyanzaru.

They were a favorite prey of the giant tigerfish, and anyone fishing for karpenta had to be wary of tigershark attacks.

Uses
A traditional Chultan way of eating kapenta was shallow-frying them whole with onions and groundnut powder.

## Kan-oji

Kan-oji was a village located in Miyama Province in Kozakura.

Description
Kan-oji was a shadow of its former self, with a population of just over two hundred people and a hundred buildings, more than half of which were abandoned and in ruins. The only factor preventing the village from becoming completely desolate was the presence of the jito of the Niwa shoen.

Trade
The village was once a bustling minor port. Its primary role used to be transporting produce from nearby estates to Kuda. However, a series of unfortunate events has led to its decline. The river from the mountains deposited silt in the harbor, severely limiting its functionality. Additionally, the construction of the Great Road has redirected much of the trade that previously passed through the village. Fishing, once a staple of the village's economy, has become increasingly difficult due to extensive mud flats that emerged with each low tide.

Government
As of, the village's jito was Kishimoto Endai. He was not particularly popular among the hyakusho

Defenses
Kishimoto Endai maintained a retinue of three samurai and ten bushi.

History
Recently, the village has faced a new and sinister threat. Malevolent creatures – including bakemono, evil hengeyokai, goblin rats, and oni – began stealthily descending from the mountains into Kan-oji. They used the derelict buildings as their bases, remaining inactive during the day and emerging at night to prey on unsuspecting victims. Their actions were so clandestine that there have been no direct witnesses or evidence of their presence.

Kishimoto Endai, along with the villagers, started to sense that something was amiss, although they lacked concrete proof. Kishimoto, unfortunately, was not an effective leader and was often preoccupied with other emergencies, leaving his samurai and bushi with little opportunity to investigate these mysterious occurrences.

Appendix
References

---

## Kabe

Kabe was a village located in Miyama Province in Kozakura.

Description
Kabe was perched atop a steep bluff overlooking the white sands of Kobawan. The village, comprising ninety-three buildings, was strategically positioned where a small stream flowed into the bay. Fishing boats, the heart of the village's livelihood, were hauled onto the beach and secured to posts in the sand. Accessibility to the beach was a feat in itself, being achieved either through a winding trail or a staircase, the latter constructed at a considerable expense to the villagers.

Trade
The villagers of Kabe faced significant hardship. Their obligation to support not only Niwa Sutai and his samurai but also the seamen and bushi under his command placed a heavy burden on their resources. Consequently, unlike other fishing villages, the people of Kabe lived in a constant state of near-poverty, struggling to meet these demands while trying to sustain their own livelihoods. This precarious economic situation set them apart from other communities.

Government
One of the village's key roles was the managerial headquarters for the Niwa estate. As of, the village's jito was Niwa Sutai.

Defenses
Niwa Sutai commanded a modest force of four samurai and ten bushi. His lineage and position in the Niwa family afforded him control over two warships, highlighting the village's strategic naval importance at the mouth of Kobawan. Each warship was staffed with ten sailors and fifteen bushi, who served as marines, and was led by a samurai captain. Typically, one ship patrolled to safeguard against pirates, while the other remained docked. The high military presence discouraged bandits.

Kamburan was a Waterdhavian merchant in the late 14 century DR.

In early spring of the, Kamburan was in debt to the moneylender Caethur and met with him and five other debtors in a room overlooking Jembril Street. He made the payment to Caethur, but was later slain by the moneylender anyway, as he was a witness to his murder of Aldurl Nael.

Kamburan had a white beard. During the meeting in which he was slain, he wore a flame-silk overtunic.

## Karran Mystamere

Dame Karran Mystamere was the Assistant to the Lord Mayor of Ravens Bluff in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Karran was an attractive and fit human woman with fair skin and green eyes. She had long, straight blonde hair. Dame Mystamere had a keen mind and talent in handling her city official position through confidence, presence, and a comforting demeanor.

Possessions
Dame Karran never parted with her amulet of proof vs detection & location. As a city official, she used a special type of ink of crimson color.

History
In her younger days, Karran Mystamere was an accomplished adventurer and one of the original members of the Knight of the Lady. She forged her reputation by being loyal to the city of Ravens Bluff, and eventually, she was granted the position of Assistant to the Lord Mayor of the Living City. By the time Dame reached middle age, she had become one of the most trusted servants of the city and was held in high regard, tasked with handling some of the more sensitive tasks by the Lord Mayor. In the, Dame Mystamere served Lady Lord Mayor Amber Lynn Thoden.

One of the sensitive assignments entrusted to Dame Mystamere was the handling of the Flight of Arrows, a Zhentarim splinter group that broke ties and betrayed the sinister organization. Its members pleaded with Ravens Bluff for an asylum. Karran handled assignments of some of the most deserts heroes of the Living City in dealing with assassination attempts by the Zhentarim on the Flight of Arrows. When members of the Flight were ensorcelled with a specific combination of the geas and curse spells, Dame Mystamere, in secrecy, tasked another group of adventurers with tracking down a famed powerful priest, secretly an archlich, Athoomb, capable of removing the magics from the Flight of Arrows, now under the protection of Ravens Bluff.

## Kathkote Dawntard

Kathkote Dawntard was a nobleman in the late 15 century DR.

Description
He sounded sarcastic even in the best of times.

Personality
He appeared to have a reputation for rage, and for drink. Nonetheless, he was smart, patient and scheming.

Relationships
He was the son of the elder Dawntard. His usual companions were Broryn Windstag and Delasko Sornstern. He was also a co-conspirator with Marlin Stormserpent's schemes, though Marlin thought he likely meant trouble.

Marlin's co-conspirators, including Dawntard, included largely families that had been ennobled recently, after the exiling of the Bleth house and the dispossession of the Cormaeril family.

Activities
He frequented the Bold Blazon.

History
By, the young Kathkote had not traveled too far from Suzail in his life. He was a guest at the Open Feast held at Castle Obarskyr on Mirtul 26 of that year.

Kathkote Dawntard got into a fight at the Dragonriders Club at some point after the Open Feast, where he'd drawn steel with his toadies and bodyguards, threatening Arclath Delcastle and slaying Delnor, a palace messenger. They were stopped by the arrival of a number of Purple Dragon knights, who threatened they would not instantly take Broryn Windstag, then drunk, at his word. The whole club was questioned thoroughly afterwards. His group fled the tavern as soon as they could, being almost late for their meeting with Marlin.

After the meeting with Marlin, Dawntard and his toadies set out on an urgent quest for a largish hand-axe, protecting their identities with nothing but revel masks, half-aware at best that what they were doing was not very strategic. This, alongside the rewards they offered in many taverns, brought them to the notice of Swordcaptain Dralkin and War Wizard Glathra Barcantle. They only succeeded in their search after Manshoon, an ancient archmage, mind-steered them towards Lord Barandror Murandrake as part of a plan of his. They were then delivered to Stormserpent Towers by magic, where, after being ushered by Marlin into a secret passage, Kathkote fainted, leaving Broryn to explain they'd found the axe.

Word of their doings eventually got to their fellow noble Arclath Delcastle by way of the dancer Amarune Whitewave.

## Kaleidoscope

A kaleidoscope was a type of optical instrument and toy in the Realms.

Description
Kaleidoscopes resembled a spyglass and were typically made from brass. But rather than clear glass, their tubes sported an array of colored glass and gems that were mirrored back at the observer, creating a vivid multicolor display.

Availability
Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold kaleidoscopes that were imported from Calimshan. Advertising it as a toy great for all ages, the catalogue offered them at a price of 50 gold pieces.

Notable Kaleidoscopes
 Black blood kaleidoscope, an artifact created by the binder Zceryll.So little is known about this artifact that it isn't even guaranteed that it is a literal kaleidoscope.
 Uxma's kaleidoscope, a Maztican kaleidoscope that was enchanted with pluma magic.

History
The existence of kaleidoscopes on Toril dated as far back as the Netherese Empire. During that time they were occasionally used by mages—in place of telescopes, glass eggs, or the far more common option of mirrors—as a medium through which to peer into other worlds or planes of existence.

Katerina Ladon is an Italian illustrator and concept artist. She has worked with companies such as Wizards of the Coast, Penguin Random House, Mana Project Studio, Paizo Inc., Ghostfire Gaming, and Monte Cook Games, among others.

## Kadeln Parosper

Kadeln Parosper was the tome-priest of Mystra in the House of the Ladystar during the mid–8 century DR.

Description
During the Chaos, he was caught at least once wearing a helm improperly sized for him.

History
About two tendays into the Chaos of the Art in the, Kadeln Parosper came to a balcony of the House of the Ladystar upon hearing the Archmage of the Phoenix Tower, Tenthar Taerhamoos, was looking for him. He gave the man a warning that they knew not the source of the Chaos of the Art, and mentioned fears that Mystra may have died ere he was pulled away, as the mob was hearing him. As Tenthar turned to leave, the archmage was forced to attack the mob with a deadly spell to gain himself the freedom to escape. Two of the people in the mob, who appeared to have been slain by his chain lightning spell, later rose, and spoke, revealing themselves to be Mystra and Azuth to the priests and onlookers before disappearing. As a sign, one of the pikes used to keep away the mob appeared in Kadeln's hand. In response, he fainted.

## Kass

Kass was a vampire spawn Gur child from the city of Baldur's Gate during the late 15 century DR.

Description
Kass was a human girl with hair streaked with white.

History
In the, Ulma led her monster hunter tribe to the city of Baldur's Gate, where they found themselves targeted by vampires. During the Absolute crisis, when the Gurs camped in the Rivington district of the city, along with a small army of refugees displaced by the conflict, the tribe's children were abducted by Astarion Ancunín under orders from Cazador Szarr.

Posters with charcoal drawings of Kass' face were found in the Lower City, promising a reward for any information on the child's fate. Kass, along with other abducted Gur children, was turned into a vampire spawn to be used in a hellish ritual by Cazador. Subsequently, Astarion Ancunín found himself broken out from under Cazador's control and, along with the Absolute Adversaries, sought to infiltrate his old master's home. The outcome of Astarion's infiltration and the fate of the Gur children remained unconfirmed.

## Karsus's Keep

Karsus's Keep was a Netherese ruin near Westgate, northwest of Starmantle. It had once held the most powerful of Karsus's spells.

Location
It stood west of Starmantle, at the end of a turn in the road that had since been reclaimed by the woods, away from the coast road between Ripplestones and Starmantle. There was a deserted town at the end of that road, and a small clearing where the remains of coaches and wagons had become covered in creepers by spring of the, suggesting that caravans had once made stops there. The place was surrounded by shadowtop trees, and there was at least one hiexel. The keep itself stood at the top of a slope or hill.

Interior
One stone platform outside was engraved with a "K"; the stone was about one man-height by two man-heights in size.

Most of the structure had been rendered completely unusable, and all archways had lost their doors, but some of the ceilings survived. There was one bedroom that had walls with gaps through which the forest was visible, strongly implying it was on the outer edge of the structure; these gaps were large enough for a man to enter, if he clambered. A few pieces of furniture remained in approximate shape, including a rotting, gilt canopied bed, a lounge, a grand table and an oval mirror. More importantly, the rear of the bedroom opened into a ring-shaped parapet with knee-high walls over a dark pit; this part of the structure was still roofed solidly enough to remain dark until the Keep's ultimate destruction. There were two phandar trees, twisted and close by, near one of the rents in the bedroom's walls.

The well had handholds and footholds. Down the well there was a natural cavern, where a throne room held a wizened corpse; this was the body of a clone of Karsus, an infamous Netherese arcanist. There were seven rune circles before the throne, arranged in a semicircle like the prow of a boat. The area nearest the throne had stone slabs for a floor, rather than natural stone.

One room, accessible through a translocating stone platform, was a low-ceilinged room lit by red light, which held entirely too many enchanted devices, making it almost knee-deep to wade through. There was a platform with a "K" on its dimmest corner.

Atmosphere
The space down the well was damp and dusty, but trails of salt covered some stones, deposited by natural processes over the ages.

One particular room, used to hold enchanted objects, was lit only by a red radiance.

Defenses
Beyond the small town outside, there was a moat with a drawbridge and stone pillars within the ditch, which had once buttressed the walls. The bridge sagged, but remained usable until the keep was destroyed.

The bedroom had wards upon the well, which attacked those who descended. There was an illusion that would trigger when people approached the rune circles and throne, but it was only bluster and threats by Karsus, who had not added actual wards to the throne room.

One room was accessible through a stone platform outside the Keep which was engraved with a "K". It was enspelled to translocate those who stepped upon it and spoke aloud "Karsus". However, the platform did not register as magical to detection. The room could only be left in the same way; there was a floating sword as its only visible guardian.

History
The place was originally used by Karsus, who hid a clone in a secluded underground throne room and prepared rune circles describing the casting of several of his most powerful spells, including one to heal all ills, and most notably including one Azuth had personally described as expressly forbidden by Mystra herself, almost certainly implying it was Karsus's avatar.

At some point, Saeraede Lyonora, an undead Netherese sorceress, arrived at the Keep, finding Karsus's clone dead. Saeraede then began her depredations across the nearby area, rendering a swath of forest into the Dead Place. She also gathered gowns in a ruined wardrobe from the caravans she exterminated nearby. Over time, she made a decision to use the Keep as a lure for mages, whose lifeforce she had a craving for. She altered the rune circles so that they would only activate for her, and then settled on Elminster Aumar, a Chosen of Mystra, as the one she wanted to lure after overhearing a conversation about him in Westgate, where she'd ranged further. She later successfully lured him into the keep.

However, the ruins were also located by Sharran Dreadspells led by Elryn, who were out to slay Elminster; they accidentally revealed the location of the Keep to the elven mage Ilbryn Starym, who was also seeking to hunt down and slay Elminster; he hid himself in the throne room and waited for a chance to ambush the wizard, while the Dreadspells found an entrance into a cache of magical devices, which they promptly looted. After entering the throne room, Elminster was successfully deceived by Saeraede Lyonora into lowering his defenses, allowing her to nearly strike him down. Azuth appeared then, accompanied by three mages: Caladaster Daermree, Tabarast, and Beldrune. Azuth warned him that what he was doing was forbidden, and that he was doomed if he didn't break free of her. At that time, the Sharrans revealed themselves, and hurled an onslaught of destructive spells from a gap in the ceiling.

Though Saeraede was able to absorb most of the magic released, the cavern was riven, and the runes holding Karsus' greatest magics were shattered. In turn, Azuth protected the three wizards with him; after the Sharrans decided they'd hurled as many spells as could be useful, they fled, noticing Azuth was still standing. In turn, Saeraede returned herself to life after draining Elminster, only to be immediately struck down by Ilbryn Starym. She then exploded in the already precarious ruins of the keep, leaving the whole chamber open to the elements; Azuth disappeared with Elminster, leaving the place to the three mages that had accompanied him. These three mages, after a short talk, discovered Saeraede's gowns, which they took with themselves.

## Karie Leddle

Karie Leddle was a Morninglord of Amaunator that visited the Wheloon Prison in Cormyr in the late 15 century DR during the Shadovar invasion of Wheloon.

Description
Karie was a light-skinned human with long black hair. She wore a set of decorated chain mail, and carried a buckler and mace-like holy symbol of Amaunator.

Personality
Karie was disgusted by the conditions of the Wheloon Prison, and refused to let the situation go unanswered.

History

Karie Leddle visited Wheloon alongside other Morninglords like Adara Semple, Dai Rolno, and Maribe Banro. She could be found at the docks of the Prison, preaching to (or shouting at) the nearby Purple Dragons, War Wizards, magistrates, and dockworkers about the mistreatment and innocence of those held within. She also worked with the Wheloon natives of Harvest Hall, and knew ways to sneak into the city through the drainage culverts with or without the permission of Cormyr.

## Karas

Karas was a cultist of the Spinner of Shadows in the world of Eberron.

Description
Karas had gray skin and white hair. He wore a chain shirt and wielded a pair of envenomed scimitars.

History

During the Darkening of Faerûn, Karas served as a leader in the Lords of Dust cult dedicated to the demonic being known as the Spinner of Shadows. As part of a plot to free their patron, Karas and the rakshasa Gnomon prepared a ritual in their Stormreach hideout to gain access to the hidden Spinner's Prison in Khyber. When the Church of the Silver Flame discovered them and attacked their hideout, Karas stood back allowed Gnomon to die first before engaging in battle himself. When he too fell, his sacrifice finished the ritual and the path to the Spinner's Prison was opened for his allies.

## Karen Court

Karen Court was a major street that crossed the entirety of The Shutters neighborhood in the Crow's End District of Ravens Bluff from east to west.

Geography
Karen Court ran parallel to Hammerstone Street and Red Sails Way, that formed The Shutters's southern border. It began at Mraedara's Street in the east and crossed Stonekeep Concourse, Burning Shield Lane, Landleen Lane, Helhavryn's Walk, Castles Way, and Deepaxe Way, reaching its end at the muddy bank of the Fire River.

Notable Locations
Fish Tails, a fried fishcake factory, two doors west from The Last Jack near the Ravensbridge.
Fruits & More, a grocer located next-door to Muldryn's Chainworks on Karen Court. It specialized in sugars, jams, and compotes, as well as crude berry-infused cordials.
The Last Jack, a quiet inn with a safe taproom located on the riverside where Stonekeep Concourse and Mraedara's Street crossed.

## Kansif

Kansif was a half-orcish wizard-evoker, a worshiper of the Absolute, and a guide, stationed in the Moonrise Towers during the late 15 century DR.

Description
Kansif was a half-orcish man with a strong body and a hide of muddy gray. He had short brown hair and wore a set of enchanted thermoarcanic gloves.

Abilities
As a mage, Kansif specialized in fire magic, dispensing firebolts and fireballs. He also wielded the cloud of daggers and cure wounds spells.

History
In the, Kansif was among those who joined the new religion that sprang in the Western Heartlands. He was stationed within the Moonrise Towers – the fortress controlled by the Absolute's warlord – Ketheric Thorm. Kansif was among the loyal Cult of the Absolute's guides that helped pilgrims reach the Moonrise Towers amidst the deadly shadow-cursed lands.

## Kaross

Kaross was an ambassador for the Shadovar of Netheril in the Shadowfell city of Evernight during the late 15 century DR.

Abilities
She was a powerful spellcaster in the Netherese tradition.

Activities
She resided in the Shadovar Embassy of Evernight and had a handful of shadar-kai bodyguards. She had to contend with subversive efforts targeted against her and her associates by the Thayan Enclave in the city.

One of her responsibilities was to safeguard the secrets of the Shard of Night and its use by the Netherese to spy on Neverwinter. She slew many Neverwintans who came close to learning of this.

History
Kaross was granted citizenship by the Tribunal of Evernight, which allowed her to operate safely within the city despite the many ravenous undead.

During the crisis appearance of the Cloaked Ascendancy in Neverwinter, Kaross was slain in a plot orchestrated by the Red Wizard Myros in which a hero from Neverwinter was sent to kill her and claim her essence. The hero did so, and unwittingly negated the protection that had been granted to them by the Tribunal upon killing the likewise protected Kaross.

## Karissa the Knife

Master Thief Karissa the Knife was a skilled rogue and senior officer of the Night Brigade gang in Wheloon Prison in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Karissa had pale skin, gray eyes, and short black hair, and wore a suit of black leather armor with a cutout on her chest. She spoke with a rough, informal voice that indicated a lifetime on and under the streets.

Personality

Karissa was aloof and solitary regarding the wider world and even towards the rest of the Night Brigade, and considered her tunnel network and gang of thieves the only important thing in her life. She exceptionally greedy and covetous, and her bedroom was also her treasure vault, and she slept in her bed with gold coins trailed from her main pile of coins. She was happy to lair in her isolated hideout and wait for visitors or invaders to struggle their way in to find her.

Possessions

Karissa's hideout and home was an opulent mansion filled with paintings, fine furniture, sculptures, alcohols, shelves full of books and scrolls, and miscellaneous trinkets and artworks. Inside her bedroom/vault, she kept many treasures including a huge drow tapestry from Sschindylryn, Cult of the Dragon masks, a statue of Amaunator, ornate weapons, and of course massive piles of currency and gemstones.

Among Karissa's treasure were her special goggles, the poniard of pain, echoes of night, tunneler's toolkit, and packrat's rat pack.

History

As the best thief in the Night Brigade, Karissa operated a thieves' guild of sorts in Wheloon Prison. Once secure in her position, she set up an opulent mansion for herself and blocked it off from any regular access. The only way to get in was to traverse a lengthy series of underground tunnels and chambers, filled with traps and monsters that she laid out for visitors. When the Riverside Rats came for their revenge against the Night Brigade, they sent allies ahead to bypass the hazards and face the deadly rogue. Surviving such threats as a ballista and otyugh, the invaders finally found Karissa in her lair and slew her.

## Kalix

Kalix was a thri-kreen ranger active in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Kalix's chitinous carapace was blue and silvery in color and lined with spikes. He had pink accents on his antennae and elsewhere, and lighter markings that evoked a lotus theme. He decorated his body with twisted vines that supported leaves, lotus blooms, and blue crystalline plates. He used lotus seed pods as shoulder pads.

Personality
Kalix saw the world as a hunting ground, and everyone he met as either predator or prey. He was cold and calculating, but willing to change his mind. He respected those who could prove their might, and was eager to explore Toril and beyond.

Abilities
Kalix was an exceptionally talented hunter and tracker, and a master of traditional thri-kreen weapons. As a thri-kreen, he possessed potent psionic talent, which he used to telepathically project himself to assist allies in battle, even at a great distance. He was particularly skilled in hunting humanoids, constructs, oozes, and celestials.

Possessions
Kalix wielded both a chatkcha and a gythka, which he named Ka'eth'rek and Drak'en'raa, respectively. He possessed various precious gemstones to use as psionic foci. He also collected trophies from his many hunts, ranging from the mundane to the exotic. Kalix owned a map of the Shaar region, which he'd charted himself. He was the keeper of the Lyric of the Eternal Lotus for an unknown amount of time.

History
Kalix was a member of the nomadic Krakk't tribe of thri-kreen. He was exiled for attacking emissaries of other groups at the Hill of Memories, and in turn became a wandering hunter for hire.

On his journeys, he explored and mapped the Shaar and became known to the nomads there.

Kalix was given a solbar gythka as payment to hunt a Calishite sultan. He was later hunted by another Calishite out of revenge, and melted down the failed hunter's skysteel weapon to forge a chatkcha for himself.

At some point during his exile, he found the Lyric of the Eternal Lotus under mysterious circumstances and became its keeper.

## Kaurdyl

Kaurdyl was a mildly toxic spice in the Realms.

Use
Kaurdyl was a spice. However, per Anastra Syluné Silverhand, it was deadly to mix huge doses of it with specific oils.

History
In, Alaithe, a servant, served her master Blandras Nuin (actually a transformed Auvrarn Labraster) a plate of morning broth with kaurdyl in it. Smelling it, he leapt to the conclusion she tried to kill him, and murdered her instead.

## Karlae

Karlae was a maid in Neverwinter in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
The young maid had a noticeably muscled back.

Personality
The woman was notably idiotic.

History
In the, she and fellow maid Nalambra was hired by Auvrarn Labraster, who was spell-disguised as Blandras Nuin, after he slew his maid Alaithe.

On the last day they worked for him, he arrived wet and soiled from a late evening of drinking, to find them holding garden shears and fire-tongs, which they had no reason to be using. They fled at his screams. He took a bath immediately, and when he called for them to bring him hot rocks, they screamed and made a racket; he'd later find them undressed in his bed, with their hair combed, after being confronted by the ghost of Anastra Syluné Silverhand, who was pretending to be the ghost of the maid Alaithe.

He leapt between them, waking and startling them; they ran out the door, tripping on each other, and then down the hallway, where the ghost ensorcelled them into a deep sleep, and then made their bodies hover, open-eyed yet knocked out, in a stiff position, outlining their shapes with dancing lights to better haunt him. That spooked him alright.

Afterwards, as the Simbul arrived, she made a point of making the house explode; however, she spared Karlae and Nalambra from the explosion, leaving them unharmed at the end of the ruckus.

He had burning yellow eyes.

Besides his abilities as a wight, he had been endowed with the ability to command the undead dragons Oskurouh and Larghauseth.

Karnal remained at the Temple of the Dragon Oracle during the early 1370s DR, as a guardian for the dignity of its dead dragons.

Kathul was a senior Red Wizard charged with subverting the partway-reborn dracolich Karliath, found in an old Cult of the Dragon base in the depths of Myth Drannor. ## Kaylie

Librarian Kaylie was an elven librarian associate of the electrum dragon Jurin'ildren in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Kaylie had light skin and long brown hair, and wore a white and blue outfit.

History
Around the, Kaylie worked with the dragon Jurin'ildren to protect an intact library in the ruins of Myth Drannor. Unfortunately, just as the Elves of Cormanthor arrived to reclaim the city, planar fluctuations caused rifts to the Elemental Plane of Fire to draw fiery creatures into the great treehouse, turning it into the Burning Library. While Jurin'ildren's abilities could keep the contents of the library safe for a time, Kaylie desperately needed some hands to get rid of the intruders before they annihilated the whole tree. Meeting Jurin'ildren's help at the entrance, she directed them to the different areas of the library to find Nepri, T'Zull, and Thulkaan while guarding the entrance and helping maintain the magical defenses.

## Kaeroven Yuluth

Kaeroven Yuluth was the human owner of the Thirsty Sailor, a seedy tavern in Waterdeep. He was also known as Smiles, though the nickname was ironic, for he smiled only rarely. The only times he did, as far as anyone else had ever seen, were when he disposed of the Thirsty Sailor's freshest corpses, throwing them down the Smugglers' Runs sewers after dragging the bodies to the back.

Description

Kaeroven was a man of disagreeable appearance; his small eyes were recessed, emitting only the most piercing and uncomfortable of gazes, and they were oft likened to the twin points of drawn daggers. He cut an imposing figure, towering and heavyset as he was. Though his curls were blond, the unmistakable lines of razor nicks on his clean-shaven face made him all the more off-putting.

He was also known to don, near-constantly, an inscribed apron. The fact that it was smothered in both blood and the cheap scent of women's perfume did Smiles little favor; whatever the reasoning behind the smell, it seemed a mystery to all.

Possessions
Among the many oddities surrounding Kaeroven was the enchanted ring he wore. Though the exact effects were unconfirmed, the mage Volo suspected it to be a variant ring of regeneration—one with vampiric qualities. This he surmised after seeing the effects on Smiles' injuries during his involvement in a fight.

Relationships
Kaeroven was known to have a frequent accomplice in the man known as Winestab, who served as a spy both within and without the walls of the Thirsty Sailor. He was Smiles's lackey in more ways than one, however, and often performed other errands for the tavernmaster. Besides him, and besides the employees of the Thirsty Sailor, Kaeroven was also known to occasionally hire criminals.

This latter sort were typically robbers and thugs, people whom he assigned duties of an even more illicit sort: to knock someone around, or even steal something if he needed to get his hands on it. Despite this, Smiles' demands were not truly his own. When he orchestrated such crimes, it was because he himself had been paid to do so, likely by a wealthier patron who wished not to be associated with these base delinquencies.

In fact, Kaeroven was a recognized contact for one such patron: the Kraken Society. Though the group did not fear getting its hands dirty, its role as an information network across the North necessitated the cooperation of individuals to gather said information in the first place. This was the sort of role that Kaeroven fulfilled.

Rumors & Legends
Much like the tavern he owned, Kaeroven was infamous among the citizens of the Dock Ward, and many of them believed him to have ties to smugglers. Smuggling was how he made most of his coin, according to those very same rumors. It was left unsaid to whom, precisely, he was linked, but some alleged that it was to Calimshan and its slave traders. Local talk went as far as to say that Smiles would kidnap members of the citizenry to be sold and used as Calishite slaves; whether or not this was true, many who openly quarreled with or slighted the man disappeared soon after.

## Kartoeba

Kartoeba, or the "Thing in the Pit", was an ageless abominable ooze creature that was believed to be a unique being on the world of Mystara, but one such creature was known to be found on Toril.

Description
These creatures were nightmarish abominations: mounds of green acidic ooze with  tentacles and a single gaping, ever-hungry maw.

Behavior
Kartoeba was a mindless creature guided by its basic desire to eat. Despite its size, it moved soundlessly and with equal ease across floors and walls, leaving only an occasional smear of green slime where it passed.

Abilities
Any creature that faced the much-maligned "Thing in the Pit" for the first time was taken over by primal terror, sending those of weaker morale into a fear-induced panic.

Combat
In battle, Kartoeba lashed with four tentacles that bludgeoned its victims and left them burned with acid. Those enemies who were unlucky enough to be entangled by a tentacle were swiftly dragged into the creature's mouth to be immediately devoured. The tentacles pulled victims at the speed of  per minute. The creature's body and tentacles always secreted corrosive digestive acids, similar to those of black puddings, but stronger.

Ecology
Kartoeba inhabited damp, sunless underground pits. If provided with sustenance, Kartoeba would not even consider leaving its comfortable cavern. It never left underground in daylight, but under the cover of night, it could slither out to the surface in search of food.

History
Kartoeba was an ancient monster that guarded the Hutaakan Temple of Pflarr on the world of Mystara. On Toril, a singular Thing in the Pit inhabited a ruin, long-lost by the 14 century DR, in the Flooded Forest in the Vast. The ruin was once a temple to Bane, the god of tyranny. A document discovered by Ombedor Steen, stated that the ruin was the first temple dedicated to Bane after his apotheosis. The deity personally established the temple before the Flooded Forest became flooded and corrupted, when it was still part of Cormanthor. Kartoeba comfortably lived in the depths of the temple's damp pits, feeding on the church's sacrifices. When the temple fell to ruin, Kartoeba began stalking the temple's sewage tunnels.

## Kaylan's wooden tray

Kaylan's wooden tray was a serving tray with an enchantment that would turn it into a temporary campfire, suitable both as a source of warmth and cooking.

Description
This tray was made of wood and its dimensions were  x .

Powers
Once per day, by speaking a command word Kaylan's wooden tray would ignite and burn with the flames of a small campfire for a period of eight hours. These flames posed no permanent damage to the tray itself, allowing it to be used indefinitely, and could be extinguished by reuttering the command word.

Upon command this  tray will burn as a normal small campfire for eight hours per day, capable of being used for cooking or warmth. It will extinguish on command and can be used indefinitely. Costs 1,500.

## Karvin's and Thurnan's Oven

Services
Rather than being a proper restaurant or tavern, Karvin's and Thurnan's Oven served handfoods to people on the street from out of a serving shutter. One of the handfoods they were known to offer was the Tantrasan classic baeranth. Like many shops of its type, the business was open from highsun to dusk.

## Kavita

Kavita was a wizard member of the Desai tribe, part of the nomadic Bedine, living in Anauroch, in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Kavita was very beautiful, with smooth brown skin, thick, long raven-black hair, and dark eyes.

History
Kavita was a Bedine wizard, who practiced her Art in secret, as the Netherese forbade the Bedine to practice magic. She worshiped the goddess At'ar the Merciless, the Yellow Goddess of the scorching desert sun.

She gave birth to her only child, Ruqiah, on the spring equinox, Ches 19, in the .

In the, Ruqiah caught the attention of the Netherese lord Parise Ulfbinder, who sent Shadovar agents Untharis and Alpirs De'Noutess to kidnap her. During the assault, Kavita was gravely wounded as she was stabbed by one of the Netherese, prompting the five-year-old Ruqiah to kill the attackers and heal her mother. Soon after, Ruqiah left to protect her parents from the Netherese.

Kavita would later train Ruqiah in the Art, among them several potent spells to control fire.

Relationships
Ruqiah
Kavita had a close relationship with her daughter, and continued supporting her after learning of her nature as Chosen of Mielikki in the .

## Kars

Kars was a human ranger and member of the Three Coins Fellowship, an adventuring company active in the Delimbiyr Vale in the late 14 century DR, alongside Douver and Eman.

History
Only newly formed in the summer of the, the Three Coins Fellowship visited the city of Loudwater in early Eleasis. They had heard the story of a ghost that wandered Tragedy's Grotto after each Shieldmeet and of how those who followed it were said to find great treasure, or else sorrow and doom. They, of course, wanted the treasure, and were driven largely by this greed. Strangely, they'd heard this story from a feminine voice in their heads, one calling itself Tymora, the goddess of luck, and promising enough riches to become kings. They had little plan beyond that.

In the streets of Loudwater late one night, the trio encountered the ghost for themselves, finding it to be the spirit of a robed monk. It ignored them and they followed it to Tragedy's Grotto and the hoped-for treasure. When another band of adventurers came across them, the trio had no desire to share and would refuse conversation and assistance with increasing vehemence, with Kars escalating to spitting threats and foul language and warning shots from his bow.

One way or another, the Three Coins Fellowship and the other adventurers followed the ghost to Tragedy's Grotto. There, it sunk into the ground and the buried vault there. Then both groups were ambushed by groundlings in Zhentarim employ, with Kars, Douver, and Eman being targeted as they were first to arrive.

If they survived the battle, Kars, Eman, and/or Douver joined the other adventurers in exploring a buried vault the ghost had entered, finding no treasure but the mysterious shard of the Fallen One. However, one of the three would become crazed, being possessed by a magical force of lawful and evil nature and attacked the others to seize the shard, whatever the cost. If subdued and questioned, he would be confused and have no memory of it. They might be persuaded to speak of the voice they'd heard, but if forcefully interrogated or magically compelled to explain why he'd attacked the others, he would be possessed again, screeching fiendishly the cryptic words "Because the key is mine!" He would then be choked to death from the unseen force; only a protection from evil or protection from law spell could prevent it. Should he be rescued, he would reveal a cruel and evil presence had been in his mind. The other adventurers took the shard and followed the trail of its mystery to the Phantoms' Cloister.The identity of this apparent possessing force is unknown—the module says this will be explained in the second part, Secret of Phantoms' Cloister, but this was never published. Being lawful evil and "fiendish", it may be a devil.

Possessions
Kars fought with a masterwork composite longbow and masterwork shortsword, and wore studded leather armor. He also had a potion of cat's grace.

Abilities
He was a swift and accurate archer.

## Karl's shield

Karl's shield was an historically important, enchanted shield during the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
Karl's shield was decorated with the device of Karl von Stag Falls. After the release of the vampire Romanisk Vlaensk, however, the device was painted over with Vlaensk's own heraldry.

Powers
The shield was unremarkable in its powers, and only its historical and political significance granted it notoriety.

History
The shield had belonged to Karl von Stag Falls, and remained in his home until the times of his great-grandson Vilhelm von Stag Falls, during the mid-to-late 14 century DR. After Vilhelm accidentally released the vampire Romanisk, who mind-controlled him and had him twist the minds of his entire estate, the device was painted over with the Vlaensk heraldry.

Notably, even if Baron Vilhelm was slain and his mind-control powers broken, none of his guards would allow anyone to remove Karl's shield, despite its usefulness in battle.Unlike other magic items in the organized play, no certificate is provided for Karl's shield. This renders it administratively impossible for the shield to be held on to by any player characters for use in later adventures.

## Karsh Garmish

Karsh Garmish was a member of the Gatekeepers in the world of Eberron.

Description
Karsh was an orcish man with gray-green skin.

History
During the Darkening of Faerûn, Gatekeeper Karsh was one of many who arrived to investigate the Spinner's Prison following the escape of the Spinner of Shadows. They found the Rift Between Worlds, a massive portal to the Demonweb Pits, and struggled to understand its nature while attempting to prevent it from threatening Eberron. Karsh and Zumosk Var lent their efforts to trying to magically seal the Rift, but their efforts could barely slow its growth.

At a time corresponding to the mid-to-late 14 century DR, Katerina Vokasich's son, Jebbrie, was bitten by spiders carrying the curse of a werespider. ## Kara Patwin

Kara Patwin was a Harper agent who worked in Eveningstar during the 1480s DR.

Description
Kara had dark skin, shoulder-length black hair, and wore a green dress.

History
Around the time of the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, Kara and Lorant Tass followed up on Oriphaun Huntsilver's activities in the Storm Horns, where he was investigating Shadovar plots. The pair were unusual for Harpers in that they were known to the officials of Cormyr, allowing them to work together without subterfuge. Unfortunately, most of those they spoke too simply dismissed their reports as overblown reactions to typical orcish activities and not worth pursuing. They were joined in this mission by Nevyle Dunwaite, Rina the Keen-Blade, Renton Finne, and Kyra Armond, and worked with Swordcaptain Orin Arroway and Sherrod Starym to unravel what was happening. After much effort, it was discovered that not only were the Shadovar raising an army in the Storm Horns, they had found an intact ancient mythallar. They planned to use it with Amskar's enclave to attack Cormyr, but Oriphaun and his allies managed to crash the flying fortress before that happened.
